Kintsugi work log: a month of work, in the open

Every page and feature shipped since the project began, between 3 Oct and 30 Oct 2026, with the time it took and what it would cost at the Kintsugi rate. Built from git history.

153items shipped
486 hwall-clock hours (estimate, at most 18 a day)
$9,720pay at the Kintsugi rate ($20 per hour, paid in BSV), about 571 BSV at $17.01/BSV on 30 Oct 2026
$27,526estimated value of work: 1,376 hours of work at $20, across parallel sessions. What a developer creates over and above the pay.

How Kintsugi works. Work is written up as tasks that an AI-assisted coder can pick up with Cursor, GitHub and a Claude account. The rate is $20 per hour, paid in BSV at the BSV/USD rate on the day of payment.

Tap any job to see an example contract for it: the offer, the terms, who commissioned it (The Bitcoin Corporation Ltd) and the paymail of the developer who did it.

This log is the founder's own work, counted at up to 18 hours a day. It comes from before the 8-hour daily cap that applies to Kintsugi developers.

Every figure here is an estimate, derived from git timestamps. It is not an invoice, a valuation or a claim.

Start here, brand and campaigns

Item · tap to see an example contractTime takenAt the Kintsugi rate
bWalletX: the smart social super wallet/bWalletX is the smart social super wallet: your token, your payments and your rooms in one place.LiveOn X10 h53 sessions · 26 days elapsed11 PRs, median 0 h open to merge$208~12.3 BSV pay$590 estimated value of work

Example contract — shows what a Kintsugi contract for this job looks like. Not signed, not an invoice.

Contract
KS-2026-0001
Client
The Bitcoin Corporation Ltd, company no. 16735102, registered in England & Wales. Commissioned the work.
Developer
b0ase · paymail b0asex@bwalletx.com ($b0asex)
Review
Kintsugi, run by The Bitcoin Corporation Ltd
Task
bWalletX: the smart social super wallet bwalletx.com/bWalletX is the smart social super wallet: your token, your payments and your rooms in one place.
Estimate
10 h (wall-clock)
Price
Client pays $261 (10.4 h × $25): $208 to the developer (10.4 h × $20, ~12.3 BSV at $17.01/BSV) and $52 to Kintsugi as its fee. Paid in BSV at the BSV/USD rate on the day of payment, both from the client's wallet in one transaction.
Estimated value of work
$590
Started
3 Oct 2026
Delivered
29 Oct 2026

Terms

  1. Payment is peer to peer: the client pays the developer directly, wallet to wallet. Kintsugi never holds the money.
  2. Up to 8 billable hours per day.
  3. Done means: the pull request is merged into the agreed repo, the change is live on the stated page, and the security tool results are attached.
  4. Security tools: Shannon for any change that touches sign-in, payments, keys or signing; Semgrep, gitleaks, pnpm audit and OSV-Scanner, CodeQL, OWASP ZAP (baseline scan) and Claude Code /security-review. Tool and AI usage costs are the developer's and are not billable hours.
  5. Kintsugi checks the spec and reviews the work. When the work is accepted, the client pays the developer's $handle in bWalletX, with Kintsugi's fee as a separate payment in the same transaction.
  6. Disputes: if the client and developer disagree about whether the work is done, Kintsugi reviews it and decides.
  7. Delivered work belongs to the client once it is paid for.
  8. Code under the Open BSV licence; specs and docs CC BY 4.0.
  9. All three parties sign the same contract in bChatX before work starts.
  10. This log is the founder's own work, counted at up to 18 hours a day. It comes from before the 8-hour daily cap that applies to Kintsugi developers.

Started
14 Oct 2026
Delivered
29 Oct 2026

Terms

  1. Payment is peer to peer: the client pays the developer directly, wallet to wallet. Kintsugi never holds the money.
  2. Up to 8 billable hours per day.
  3. Done means: the pull request is merged into the agreed repo, the change is live on the stated page, and the security tool results are attached.
  4. Security tools: Shannon for any change that touches sign-in, payments, keys or signing; Semgrep, gitleaks, pnpm audit and OSV-Scanner, CodeQL, OWASP ZAP (baseline scan) and Claude Code /security-review. Tool and AI usage costs are the developer's and are not billable hours.
  5. Kintsugi checks the spec and reviews the work. When the work is accepted, the client pays the developer's $handle in bWalletX, with Kintsugi's fee as a separate payment in the same transaction.
  6. Disputes: if the client and developer disagree about whether the work is done, Kintsugi reviews it and decides.
  7. Delivered work belongs to the client once it is paid for.
  8. Code under the Open BSV licence; specs and docs CC BY 4.0.
  9. All three parties sign the same contract in bChatX before work starts.

Terms

  1. Payment is peer to peer: the client pays the developer directly, wallet to wallet. Kintsugi never holds the money.
  2. Up to 8 billable hours per day.
  3. Done means: the pull request is merged into the agreed repo, the change is live on the stated page, and the security tool results are attached.
  4. Security tools: Shannon for any change that touches sign-in, payments, keys or signing; Semgrep, gitleaks, pnpm audit and OSV-Scanner, CodeQL, OWASP ZAP (baseline scan) and Claude Code /security-review. Tool and AI usage costs are the developer's and are not billable hours.
  5. Kintsugi checks the spec and reviews the work. When the work is accepted, the client pays the developer's $handle in bWalletX, with Kintsugi's fee as a separate payment in the same transaction.
  6. Disputes: if the client and developer disagree about whether the work is done, Kintsugi reviews it and decides.
  7. Delivered work belongs to the client once it is paid for.
  8. Code under the Open BSV licence; specs and docs CC BY 4.0.
  9. All three parties sign the same contract in bChatX before work starts.
  10. This log is the founder's own work, counted at up to 18 hours a day. It comes from before the 8-hour daily cap that applies to Kintsugi developers.

KS-2026-0129
Client
The Bitcoin Corporation Ltd, company no. 16735102, registered in England & Wales. Commissioned the work.
Developer
b0ase · paymail b0asex@bwalletx.com ($b0asex)
Review
Kintsugi, run by The Bitcoin Corporation Ltd
Task
Subscriptions in bWalletX bwalletx.com/subscriptionsPut money aside in a pot and pay regular amounts from it. The pot balance is the most anyone can take.
Estimate
0.6 h (wall-clock)
Price
Client pays $14 (0.6 h × $25): $11 to the developer (0.6 h × $20, ~0.66 BSV at $17.01/BSV) and $3 to Kintsugi as its fee. Paid in BSV at the BSV/USD rate on the day of payment, both from the client's wallet in one transaction.
Estimated value of work
$32
Started
7 Oct 2026
Delivered
25 Oct 2026

Terms

  1. Payment is peer to peer: the client pays the developer directly, wallet to wallet. Kintsugi never holds the money.
  2. Up to 8 billable hours per day.
  3. Done means: the pull request is merged into the agreed repo, the change is live on the stated page, and the security tool results are attached.
  4. Security tools: Shannon for any change that touches sign-in, payments, keys or signing; Semgrep, gitleaks, pnpm audit and OSV-Scanner, CodeQL, OWASP ZAP (baseline scan) and Claude Code /security-review. Tool and AI usage costs are the developer's and are not billable hours.
  5. Kintsugi checks the spec and reviews the work. When the work is accepted, the client pays the developer's $handle in bWalletX, with Kintsugi's fee as a separate payment in the same transaction.
  6. Disputes: if the client and developer disagree about whether the work is done, Kintsugi reviews it and decides.
  7. Delivered work belongs to the client once it is paid for.
  8. Code under the Open BSV licence; specs and docs CC BY 4.0.
  9. All three parties sign the same contract in bChatX before work starts.
  10. This log is the founder's own work, counted at up to 18 hours a day. It comes from before the 8-hour daily cap that applies to Kintsugi developers.

  1. Payment is peer to peer: the client pays the developer directly, wallet to wallet. Kintsugi never holds the money.
  2. Up to 8 billable hours per day.
  3. Done means: the pull request is merged into the agreed repo, the change is live on the stated page, and the security tool results are attached.
  4. Security tools: Shannon for any change that touches sign-in, payments, keys or signing; Semgrep, gitleaks, pnpm audit and OSV-Scanner, CodeQL, OWASP ZAP (baseline scan) and Claude Code /security-review. Tool and AI usage costs are the developer's and are not billable hours.
  5. Kintsugi checks the spec and reviews the work. When the work is accepted, the client pays the developer's $handle in bWalletX, with Kintsugi's fee as a separate payment in the same transaction.
  6. Disputes: if the client and developer disagree about whether the work is done, Kintsugi reviews it and decides.
  7. Delivered work belongs to the client once it is paid for.
  8. Code under the Open BSV licence; specs and docs CC BY 4.0.
  9. All three parties sign the same contract in bChatX before work starts.
  10. This log is the founder's own work, counted at up to 18 hours a day. It comes from before the 8-hour daily cap that applies to Kintsugi developers.

5 Oct 2026
Delivered

Terms

  1. Payment is peer to peer: the client pays the developer directly, wallet to wallet. Kintsugi never holds the money.
  2. Up to 8 billable hours per day.
  3. Done means: the pull request is merged into the agreed repo, the change is live on the stated page, and the security tool results are attached.
  4. Security tools: Shannon for any change that touches sign-in, payments, keys or signing; Semgrep, gitleaks, pnpm audit and OSV-Scanner, CodeQL, OWASP ZAP (baseline scan) and Claude Code /security-review. Tool and AI usage costs are the developer's and are not billable hours.
  5. Kintsugi checks the spec and reviews the work. When the work is accepted, the client pays the developer's $handle in bWalletX, with Kintsugi's fee as a separate payment in the same transaction.
  6. Disputes: if the client and developer disagree about whether the work is done, Kintsugi reviews it and decides.
  7. Delivered work belongs to the client once it is paid for.
  8. Code under the Open BSV licence; specs and docs CC BY 4.0.
  9. All three parties sign the same contract in bChatX before work starts.

Example contract — shows what a Kintsugi contract for this job looks like. Not signed, not an invoice.

Contract
KS-2026-0119
Client
The Bitcoin Corporation Ltd, company no. 16735102, registered in England & Wales. Commissioned the work.
Developer
b0ase · paymail b0asex@bwalletx.com ($b0asex)
Review
Kintsugi, run by The Bitcoin Corporation Ltd
Task
Our b*X services bwalletx.com/servicesEvery b*X.com site from bWalletX in one place: bChatX, bMailX, bCalX, bRadioX, bNewsX, bMusicX, bWriterX and more.
Estimate
0.1 h (wall-clock)
Price
Client pays $2 (0.1 h × $25): $2 to the developer (0.1 h × $20, ~0.10 BSV at $17.01/BSV) and $0 to Kintsugi as its fee. Paid in BSV at the BSV/USD rate on the day of payment, both from the client's wallet in one transaction.
Estimated value of work
$5
Started
25 Oct 2026
Delivered
25 Oct 2026

Terms

  1. Payment is peer to peer: the client pays the developer directly, wallet to wallet. Kintsugi never holds the money.
  2. Up to 8 billable hours per day.
  3. Done means: the pull request is merged into the agreed repo, the change is live on the stated page, and the security tool results are attached.
  4. Security tools: Shannon for any change that touches sign-in, payments, keys or signing; Semgrep, gitleaks, pnpm audit and OSV-Scanner, CodeQL, OWASP ZAP (baseline scan) and Claude Code /security-review. Tool and AI usage costs are the developer's and are not billable hours.
  5. Kintsugi checks the spec and reviews the work. When the work is accepted, the client pays the developer's $handle in bWalletX, with Kintsugi's fee as a separate payment in the same transaction.
  6. Disputes: if the client and developer disagree about whether the work is done, Kintsugi reviews it and decides.
  7. Delivered work belongs to the client once it is paid for.
  8. Code under the Open BSV licence; specs and docs CC BY 4.0.
  9. All three parties sign the same contract in bChatX before work starts.
  10. This log is the founder's own work, counted at up to 18 hours a day. It comes from before the 8-hour daily cap that applies to Kintsugi developers.

  5. Kintsugi checks the spec and reviews the work. When the work is accepted, the client pays the developer's $handle in bWalletX, with Kintsugi's fee as a separate payment in the same transaction.
  6. Disputes: if the client and developer disagree about whether the work is done, Kintsugi reviews it and decides.
  7. Delivered work belongs to the client once it is paid for.
  8. Code under the Open BSV licence; specs and docs CC BY 4.0.
  9. All three parties sign the same contract in bChatX before work starts.

Client pays $4 (0.2 h × $25): $4 to the developer (0.2 h × $20, ~0.21 BSV at $17.01/BSV) and $1 to Kintsugi as its fee. Paid in BSV at the BSV/USD rate on the day of payment, both from the client's wallet in one transaction.
Estimated value of work
$10
Started
8 Oct 2026
Delivered
8 Oct 2026

Terms

  1. Payment is peer to peer: the client pays the developer directly, wallet to wallet. Kintsugi never holds the money.
  2. Up to 8 billable hours per day.
  3. Done means: the pull request is merged into the agreed repo, the change is live on the stated page, and the security tool results are attached.
  4. Security tools: Shannon for any change that touches sign-in, payments, keys or signing; Semgrep, gitleaks, pnpm audit and OSV-Scanner, CodeQL, OWASP ZAP (baseline scan) and Claude Code /security-review. Tool and AI usage costs are the developer's and are not billable hours.
  5. Kintsugi checks the spec and reviews the work. When the work is accepted, the client pays the developer's $handle in bWalletX, with Kintsugi's fee as a separate payment in the same transaction.
  6. Disputes: if the client and developer disagree about whether the work is done, Kintsugi reviews it and decides.
  7. Delivered work belongs to the client once it is paid for.
  8. Code under the Open BSV licence; specs and docs CC BY 4.0.
  9. All three parties sign the same contract in bChatX before work starts.
  10. This log is the founder's own work, counted at up to 18 hours a day. It comes from before the 8-hour daily cap that applies to Kintsugi developers.

Client pays $50 (2 h × $25): $40 to the developer (2 h × $20, ~2.35 BSV at $17.01/BSV) and $10 to Kintsugi as its fee. Paid in BSV at the BSV/USD rate on the day of payment, both from the client's wallet in one transaction.
Status
Not open yet

Terms

  1. Payment is peer to peer: the client pays the developer directly, wallet to wallet. Kintsugi never holds the money.
  2. Up to 8 billable hours per day.
  3. Done means: the pull request is merged into the agreed repo, the post is live on bwalletx.com/blog and linked from the page, and the security tool results are attached.
  4. Security tools: Shannon for any change that touches sign-in, payments, keys or signing; Semgrep, gitleaks, pnpm audit and OSV-Scanner, CodeQL, OWASP ZAP (baseline scan) and Claude Code /security-review. Tool and AI usage costs are the developer's and are not billable hours.
  5. Kintsugi checks the spec and reviews the work. When the work is accepted, the client pays the developer's $handle in bWalletX, with Kintsugi's fee as a separate payment in the same transaction.
  6. Disputes: if the client and developer disagree about whether the work is done, Kintsugi reviews it and decides.
  7. Delivered work belongs to the client once it is paid for.
  8. Code under the Open BSV licence; specs and docs CC BY 4.0.
  9. All three parties sign the same contract in bChatX before work starts.

  6. Disputes: if the client and developer disagree about whether the work is done, Kintsugi reviews it and decides.
  7. Delivered work belongs to the client once it is paid for.
  8. Code under the Open BSV licence; specs and docs CC BY 4.0.
  9. All three parties sign the same contract in bChatX before work starts.

Blog post for Coming from Money Button?/restore/moneybuttonPage with no blog post yet.Not open yet2 hfixed estimate$40~2.35 BSV

Open offer: example of how this job will be offered. Not open for acceptance yet. Register interest to be told when tasks open.

Offer
KS-OFFER-0050
Client
The Bitcoin Corporation Ltd, company no. 16735102, registered in England & Wales
Developer
Open: first accepted developer
Review
Kintsugi, run by The Bitcoin Corporation Ltd
Task
Write and publish a blog post for Coming from Money Button? bwalletx.com/restore/moneybutton
Depends on
None
Estimate
2 h (fixed)
Price
Client pays $50 (2 h × $25): $40 to the developer (2 h × $20, ~2.35 BSV at $17.01/BSV) and $10 to Kintsugi as its fee. Paid in BSV at the BSV/USD rate on the day of payment, both from the client's wallet in one transaction.
Status
Not open yet

Terms

  1. Payment is peer to peer: the client pays the developer directly, wallet to wallet. Kintsugi never holds the money.
  2. Up to 8 billable hours per day.
  3. Done means: the pull request is merged into the agreed repo, the post is live on bwalletx.com/blog and linked from the page, and the security tool results are attached.
  4. Security tools: Shannon for any change that touches sign-in, payments, keys or signing; Semgrep, gitleaks, pnpm audit and OSV-Scanner, CodeQL, OWASP ZAP (baseline scan) and Claude Code /security-review. Tool and AI usage costs are the developer's and are not billable hours.
  5. Kintsugi checks the spec and reviews the work. When the work is accepted, the client pays the developer's $handle in bWalletX, with Kintsugi's fee as a separate payment in the same transaction.
  6. Disputes: if the client and developer disagree about whether the work is done, Kintsugi reviews it and decides.
  7. Delivered work belongs to the client once it is paid for.
  8. Code under the Open BSV licence; specs and docs CC BY 4.0.
  9. All three parties sign the same contract in bChatX before work starts.

  1. Payment is peer to peer: the client pays the developer directly, wallet to wallet. Kintsugi never holds the money.
  2. Up to 8 billable hours per day.
  3. Done means: the pull request is merged into the agreed repo, the post is live on bwalletx.com/blog and linked from the page, and the security tool results are attached.
  4. Security tools: Shannon for any change that touches sign-in, payments, keys or signing; Semgrep, gitleaks, pnpm audit and OSV-Scanner, CodeQL, OWASP ZAP (baseline scan) and Claude Code /security-review. Tool and AI usage costs are the developer's and are not billable hours.
  5. Kintsugi checks the spec and reviews the work. When the work is accepted, the client pays the developer's $handle in bWalletX, with Kintsugi's fee as a separate payment in the same transaction.
  6. Disputes: if the client and developer disagree about whether the work is done, Kintsugi reviews it and decides.
  7. Delivered work belongs to the client once it is paid for.
  8. Code under the Open BSV licence; specs and docs CC BY 4.0.
  9. All three parties sign the same contract in bChatX before work starts.

  7. Delivered work belongs to the client once it is paid for.
  8. Code under the Open BSV licence; specs and docs CC BY 4.0.
  9. All three parties sign the same contract in bChatX before work starts.

Terms

  1. Payment is peer to peer: the client pays the developer directly, wallet to wallet. Kintsugi never holds the money.
  2. Up to 8 billable hours per day.
  3. Done means: the pull request is merged into the agreed repo, the post is live on bwalletx.com/blog after the feature is in a release build, and the security tool results are attached.
  4. Security tools: Shannon for any change that touches sign-in, payments, keys or signing; Semgrep, gitleaks, pnpm audit and OSV-Scanner, CodeQL, OWASP ZAP (baseline scan) and Claude Code /security-review. Tool and AI usage costs are the developer's and are not billable hours.
  5. Kintsugi checks the spec and reviews the work. When the work is accepted, the client pays the developer's $handle in bWalletX, with Kintsugi's fee as a separate payment in the same transaction.
  6. Disputes: if the client and developer disagree about whether the work is done, Kintsugi reviews it and decides.
  7. Delivered work belongs to the client once it is paid for.
  8. Code under the Open BSV licence; specs and docs CC BY 4.0.
  9. All three parties sign the same contract in bChatX before work starts.

How the numbers are made

  1. Items. Every public page on bwalletx.com that is in the sitemap. Internal company matters are left out on purpose.
  2. Commits. Every commit since the project began on 3 Oct 2026, from: bwalletx.com site (887 commits), bWalletX app (1,672 commits), bChatX (635 commits), bMailX site (13 commits), bMarketX site (34 commits), bGitX site (4 commits). A site commit counts towards the pages and share images it touched. An app commit counts towards a page when a file path names that feature (for example a pots folder counts towards Pots). A commit that touches several items is split evenly between them. App commits that match no page go into "shared code". Commit messages are never published.
  3. Time taken. Commits are grouped into sessions: a gap of more than 2 hours starts a new one. Each session counts as last commit minus first commit, plus 30 minutes for the work before the first commit. Elapsed days and PR open-to-merge times are shown for context only.
  4. Cost. Hours × the Kintsugi rate of $20 an hour, paid in BSV at the BSV/USD rate on the day of payment. BSV figures are approximate, converted at $17.01 per BSV, the price fetched once from WhatsOnChain on 30 Oct 2026. This is what the same output would cost an AI-assisted coder on Kintsugi, not what it cost us.
  5. Limits. Commit gaps miss design and thinking time, and quick AI-assisted bursts look short. Treat each figure as a rough range.

Generated 30 Oct 2026 by scripts/kintsugi-log.py.
